France → Cecil and the Queen · nomenclator: homophonic alphabet, dotted-letter word codes, name signs and null words · 1563–1566

Sir Thomas Smith’s ciphered despatches from France, 1563–1566

→

British Library, Add MS 4136 ff. 140, 157–168 (DECODE R9236, R9248, R9249, R9250, R9252, R9253, R9254): Patrick Forbes’s copies of the ciphered stretches of eighteen letters. Smith’s key is on f. 179 of the same volume (DECODE R9261); applied to the copies, it decrypts most passages in full or in large part. The originals were deciphered at the time; Forbes printed the 1563 passages in 1741, and the Calendar of State Papers summarises the rest.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 21 September 2026 · updated 24 September 2026

Method: read with known key · Extent: complete

Credits · Satoshi Tomokiyo (pointer to a source) · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used) · Patrick Forbes (key or decipherment used)

known key
On this pageThe records and the key1563: the passages Forbes printed1563–1566: the passages the calendar only summarFolio 140: five earlier letters (DECODE R9236)MethodWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources
Summary. The catalogue listed six DECODE records of Sir Thomas Smith, ambassador in France from 1562, as not decrypted. They are leaves from the working file of Patrick Forbes, who in the 1730s copied only the ciphered stretches of each despatch, numbered them, and printed the decipherments in A Full View of the Public Transactions in the Reign of Q. Elizabeth (1740–41). The six records hold eighteen letters, from 17 January 1563 to 29 April 1566. Smith’s own key, “Sr Thomas Smith’s Cipher”, is filed in the same volume as DECODE R9261; Tomokiyo had pointed to it in 2024. It was applied to the passages, transcribed from the images. The five letters of January–April 1563 agree word for word with Forbes’s printed text. The thirteen later letters were deciphered at the time on the originals in The National Archives, but the Calendar of State Papers, Foreign gives only summaries; here they are decrypted in Smith’s words, most of them in full or in large part, and none contradicts the calendar. Two headings are misdated (Avignon 4 October 1564, not 27 October; 22 March 1566, not 1567), and one word the key lists among the nulls, enos, means “ambassador”. Some dotted-letter word signs are still interpreted from context.

01 The records and the key

Add MS 4136 is Forbes’s deciphering file for his Full View. For each despatch he copied only what was in cipher, numbered the stretches (1), (2), … in the order they come in the letter, and headed the group with writer, recipient and date. The clear English place names scattered through the copies (London, Dover, Hastings, Lyn, Boston, Poole, Dunkirk…) and the words and, bycause, don, yf are Smith’s nulls: the key lists them under “Nothings”.

The alphabet gives each letter from a to m one to four invented signs, and n, o and p the figures 2–4, 5–7 and 8–10. The same figures with a dot are r and s, and with a colon t, u/v and y, so 6 is o, 6. r and 6: u. Common words are Latin letters marked with dots, colons or bars on different sides (·A and, A: all, :a at, b: be, :b by, F: from, :W which, Ẅ with), and da and ba are the endings -ing and -ed. About thirty signs stand for names: the Admiral, the Constable, the Duke of Guise, the Queen Mother, the Prince of Condé, the Cardinal of Lorraine, the Queen’s Majesty, England, Scotland, France, Spain, the Pope.

02 1563: the passages Forbes printed

The first test passage, in the letter to the Queen of 17 January 1563, came out as chief … on the other side, and Forbes (ii 287) prints: “I do take yt marvelous necessary, that some man were sent to be with the admirall who now is chief on the other side.” In the letter to Cecil of 27 January all 32 stretches run with Forbes’s text in order: the rendering of Tankervill; that is to have one on the other side with the admiral, and I have named him who, for th’acquaintance with the admiral and all of that faction, the knowledge of the countrey and all other respectes, is most fit. But you must dispach him streight; you must not stic for honest allowance in tyme of service; Monsieur Danville is to come over into England shortly; such noblemen of Scotland as favoreth the religion and wold contynew the amytie between Scotland and us. The letter of 13 April is decrypted in full, the Blois letter of 7 March about half.

03 1563–1566: the passages the calendar only summarises

Arles, 24 November 1564 (“chiefly in cipher, deciphered”, CSP no. 815): “[The Cardinal of Lorraine] and the [Prince of Condé] have had conferens and familiar banketting togither at Soissons and at Nanteuil chasteau, which was the late [Cardinal] of Burbons’ … They of Guyse … complaine that all letters that come from the Cardinal … be opened, principally at Paris and Lyons … Madame de Guise … daily in great dispatches to and from Scotland … This bearer Hume is altogether a Lidington, whom you shall find double, I am afraid, or rather triple, having … pencions of all three princes.”

Avignon, 4 October 1564 (CSP no. 714; Forbes’s heading had been taken as 27 October): the two designs of the French court, one of them of the papists, to bring “all again to the olde state” and “break the amity” of England and Scotland; “the Bishop of Glasgow is here greater, no embassador”; “the marriage of the Queen of Scots”, the Earl of Warwick, Lord Robert and the Prince of Spain, “two marriages for her”; “the house of Guise stronger, which is too stronge”; the Earl of Lennox’s practice “to marry his sonne”, and “they feare that she is enclined to this last offer of Lenox”.

Valence, 1 September 1564 (CSP no. 642): the amity between England and Scotland, the Earl of Murray, “such lords and great men … be the great favourers of religion … to do some great enterprise”, Huntly “died and his sonnes were taken”, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow’s pension, and “to marry Lord Robert”. On 3 September the two ciphered words are names: Nanion Cocborne and Villeroy.

25 January 1564 (CSP no. 103): the Spanish ambassador reports the French making ladders at Étaples and Boulogne, to surprise a town in England or fall on the Low Countries; the King of Spain has made no league with the Pope and means nothing against the Queen of Navarre, but wants “to make himself rich”.

The other letters are shorter. Arles, 20 November, on the bearer Adam Hume, runs almost word for word with the calendar up to “glad to be rid of him”. Narbonne, 7 January 1565, covers the posts out of Scotland, Beton and Hume going by land, and “the marriage between the Duke of Guise and the Queen of Scots”. From Bourbon-l’Archambault, 22 March 1566, come the ambassadors of Spain and Scotland with the Cardinal of Lorraine, the Bishop of Glasgow looking to be a cardinal, and a Scottish woman who is “the conveyor of [letters] out of Scotland to the [Lady] Lennox”. And 29 April 1566 names “Peter Clare … Almain captain … Albert de Rosemberg”, and the “continuation with the princes of Almain for the recovery of Metz and Calais”.

04 Folio 140: five earlier letters (DECODE R9236)

A seventh record, R9236 (Add MS 4136 f. 140, catalogue 94), is another of Forbes’s leaves: the ciphered passages of Smith to Cecil of 15 October, 20 October and 15/17 December 1562, to the Queen of 4 November 1563, and of “Sir Thomas Smith’s advertisements from France, 31 Jan. 1562[/3]”. The clear words scattered through them (London, Lyn, Poole, Boston, Dover, Hastings, Garnesey) are the nulls of the same key, R9261; DECODE names R9262 as the nearest key, which is Throckmorton’s. Decryptions, added 21 September 2026:

  • 15 October 1562, all six passages: the Scots, the Queen of Scots, “break with you and to make war”, “that the hostages which you have shall be convoyed”, Flanders, Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort. These agree with Forbes, Full View ii 119.
  • 15/17 December 1562, all six: “I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to reason but compellid … to trust no Frenchman of what religion soever he be … the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe … as soon as ever they be able, they will thither [bend] all their force”. These agree with Forbes ii 238.
  • 4 November 1563, to the Queen, decrypted in full and confirmed by the calendar (CSP Foreign vi no. 1366): “…de Montaigne … whose carte of La Rochelle … under another pretence. I have given letters to my Lord Robert and Mr Secretarie … from Rouen … wrote of that same matter … I thinke he can do. But this at this tyme is most necessarie.”
  • 20 October 1562, all four passages, with Forbes ii 122 as the check: [Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence “with a garden of chamillion upon the hill on this side”; things begun there by [France] be not perfit.
  • 31 January 1562/3, the advertisements: only passage (2), “the queen of Navarre”, is decrypted; (1), (3) and the last line remain fragments, and no printed text was found to check them against.

One variant appears on this leaf only: a double bar stands for i. Passage-by-passage decryptions are in targets/smith1562/decipher_E.md.

05 Method

The six records and the Smith-series key records were fetched from DECODE. The first key record DECODE names, R9262, is Throckmorton’s third cipher. The record listing around it gave R9261, whose first page is Smith’s cipher and whose second is Sir James Croft’s. The passages were transcribed from the images and the key applied sign by sign. Forbes’s printed letters (vol. ii, from the archive.org text) and the calendar summaries were used only to confirm decryptions; where they did not fit the signs the passage was left open. Values added to the key:

  • enos, listed at the top of the key among the nulls, is “Ambassador”; a marginal gloss on the 20 November letter spells it out.
  • M̅ and M̈ are marriage and marry, B: is Bishop, C̄ is Congregation, J̣ is is or are, ! is in or into.
  • The doubled long-s signs are st, the small open v is l, and a looped kite with a stem is w.
  • A variant of the England sign stands for Earl (before Murray, Lennox and Warwick). The flag sign, the key’s “Germany” or “the Emperor”, stands where the calendar names Archduke Charles.
  • Forbes’s copying slips: a single dot for a colon, 4 for e, ⊔ for π.

06 What remains uncertain

  • The hooked-T family of word signs (here, his, they, this, thing) is interpreted from context; Forbes’s dots on it are inconsistent (C).
  • Undecrypted word signs: ẓ, ï, C·, F̄ and the ḧ-like sign in 1 September passage 2.
  • Letters decrypted only in part: 7 March 1563 (passages 2, 9, 10, 13, 14), 27 July 1563, Marseilles 13 November 1564 (3, 10), Arles 22 November 1564, 22 March 1566 (1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25, 26). The 12 March 1563 letter is in Forbes and was not transcribed.
  • Marseilles (5): the key’s sign for Calais where the calendar has Dieppe.
  • Avignon passages 22–23 (“by litle and litle and by … great”) and 8–11 have no counterpart in the calendar summary (M).

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

No. 01, Smith to the Queen, Chartres, 17 Jan 1562/3 (R9248, f. 157)

Sir Thomas Smith to the Queen, Chartres, 17 Jan 1562/3: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (spot-read only (passages 2-3: "chief on the other side"); checked against Forbes ii 287-9; CSP F. 6 no. 109). No transcription or decryption file exists for this letter.

No. 02, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 27 Jan 1562/3 (R9249, f. 158)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 27 Jan 1562/3: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all 32 passages; checked against Forbes ii 315-18). In decipher_A.md, Letter 1: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Letter 3 — Smith to the Queen, 27 July 1563
DECODE R9250 P1, Add MS 4136 f.159. Not in Forbes as such; CSP Foreign vol. 6 no. 1078 (Smith to the Queen,
Valmont 28 July 1563, §5: the Earl of Warwick's letter "does not answer whether he be able to keep it, or for what
time, nor what men he has in it"). Forbes's date 27 July may be the date of drafting; the content fits no. 1078.

## Letter 4 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4
DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher
deciphered"). Passages (1)-(2) only numbered; the block is one long stretch. Read as runs; not every group
resolved.

- (1) `464: ö{10: T. :F` — "not only the French[?]" [?]
- (2) `T ⊓⊢̈‡⊔⊓9.10.⊔ξ66. .o ⊡ ṡ 6o[?] ⊢̈L ξ67.` — "the ambassador of Spain send[s] me word"
  `:T ḥ ω⊽[?] ḥ G.` "that he hath good" `⊔ξ5:L7.3:π9.L⊢̈L24:` "advertisement" `:T T :F[?] ṁ: ⟨?⟩ ⊓{ξL7.10. ·A ∞. 9.6:γω L4.5:⊔9…` "that the French make ladders and other such equipages"

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Decryption

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

No. 03, Smith to Cecil, Blois, 7 Mar 1562/3 (R9252, ff. 163-4)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Blois, 7 Mar 1562/3: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (about half; checked against Forbes ii 350-51). In decipher_B.md, first section: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

decipher_B.md

# Smith cipher passages, BL Add MS 4136 (Forbes copy): decipherment B

Partial first pass. Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the key image R9261 p.1. Cribs were used only to
check them. `[?]` means an uncertain letter or word, and `⟨code⟩` means an unread code group. Letters marked
NOT ATTEMPTED have no reading yet.

## Smith to Cecil, Blois 7 Mar 1562/3: R9252 p.1 (lower half) and p.2 top (Forbes f.163-164)
Crib: Forbes, *Full View* vol. 2, pp. 350-353 (forbes2.txt l.29152-29406). The ciphered passages fall in the
paragraph "Ye know now what is to be done ... will not lenger be dalied withall" (p. 350).

- (1) `Hull ⌐5:L2 1 T̈ hastings M̈ {π910L / ⊤:πξL` → *[Hull null]* **even** ⟨1⟩ ⟨T̈⟩ *[null]* ⟨M̈ = matter?⟩ ⟨{π910L⟩ **t i [c?] l e**.
  Only partly read. "even" is sure (⌐=e, 5:=v, L=e, 2=n). ⊤:πξL may be "tickle" (Forbes has "so ticlish"), but ξ is a d-form in the key, so the reading is left open.
- (2) `∝γ T̈ b: and` → not read (⟨∝γ⟩ ⟨T̈⟩ be *[and null]*).
- (3) `Jersey Ẅ 7 6: 2: ∝ω⊔7·ʒL garnesey ⊢L4: ⊕ :k` → *[null]* **without charge** *[null]* **let [Admiral] know**.
  Matches Forbes "done without charge. Let the admirall know". ʒ as g is uncertain.
- (4) `Dunkirk ·h A: ⊕A· and C̈ 8·5⊢L6·` → *[null]* **have all** ⟨⊕A· = Normandy?⟩ *[and null]* ⟨C̈ = again/this?⟩ **somer**.
  Matches Forbes "that ye will have all Normandy again this somer". The ⊕A· sign is not in the key's name list as read.
- (5) `London ·1 f: T·⊢ byrause 9{π π2}10: ·1 :k` → *[null]* **I** ⟨f:⟩ ⟨T·⊢ = tell?⟩ *[null]* **plainly I know**.
  Matches "I tell them plainly, I know".
- (6) `C: ω⊔5:L` → ⟨C: = they?⟩ **have**. Matches "what captaines they have".
- (7) `C:: ⊓1 ⊓‡{L T̈ m:` → **they** [?] **a[r?] able to make**. Matches "what men ... they are able to make".
- (8) `♀-box` → **the Queen's Majesty**.
- (9) `Dunkirk f ω5ſfu⊔XL10· 76·⊔96:7·π2∝L9· f ⊖ ʕ1⊓ T̈ b: / 7·L2{6·π{:.. / 2·5:πL2:}9: G. L̈ bycause` →
  **for** ⟨...⟩ **... for [England?] ... be ...**. Not read. The crib is "now the weather doth com in propyce for it, will not lenger be dalied withall", but I could not align the glyphs to it without forcing them.
- (10) `ñ ⁚M T̈ θL9⌐ ⊸` → not read (⊸ = Newhaven sign?).
- (11) `Lyn a· C: θL95 ⊙— F: 7:10· bycause` → *[null]* ... ⟨θL9⌐ = keep?⟩ **Calais** **from** ⟨7:10·⟩. Probably the crib's "to kepe Calais from us" (p. 351). "keep" is doubtful (θ=k, L=e, 9=p gives "kep").
- (12) `Jersey T̈ ω⊓5:L ⊙— A·` → *[null]* ⟨T̈⟩ **have Calais again**. Not confirmed.
- (13) `Boston ∝52□L⊓π{ T·6·7:L⊓θ3410· ·A / T7· 2Lξ·o·∞ □6·L2ξ10·ωπ98⌐ Dover C: ‡L∝π2 35o·o / T̈ 8·L⊔4 C6:M garnesey` → **...friends...** ⟨mostly unread⟩. The fragment □6·L2ξ10· reads "frends" (friendship?). Crib: "they have ... confessid ... ther nede of our frendshippe".
- (14) `·h obiM̈f·1 C8·ʕ·A T̈ ʕ London` → not read.
- (15) `Hull ·o ⊳` → **of [Queen of Scots]**. Matches "the entermeadling of the queen of Scots".
- (16) `♀-box π10` → **the Queen's Majesty** ⟨π10⟩.

## Smith to Cecil, Valence 1 Sep 1564: R9252 p.2 (Forbes f.164)
Symbols only in (1)-(3), (5), (6): ⩓ Scotland, ♇-like sign ⟨code⟩, 凸 England.
- (4) `T· π⊢π2:9: / ⊢5:5·6·⊓8:` → **the** ⟨...⟩. Not read.
- (8) `8·7:∝ꟿ :L ·A C̈ M·` → **such** ⟨:L⟩ **and** ⟨C̈⟩ **men**.
- (9) `b: T· G̈ □⊔6:5·L6·9· ·o 5·L{πxπ62` → **be the great[?] fav[ou]rers of religion**. Sure except G̈.
- (10) `T̈ θ· & C̈ L22:L6·87·π9·L .C {π9:L` → ... **enterprise** ⟨.C {π9:L⟩.
- (12) `·o T· 5·L{πxπ62` → **of the religion**.
- (13) `f M̈ C̈:L·o` → **for many**[?] ⟨...⟩.
- (11), (14)-(31): not read.

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Letter 2 — Smith to Cecil, 13 Apr 1563
DECODE R9250 (IMG_R9250_I43261_P1, top), Add MS 4136 f.159. Printed: Forbes vol. II pp. 384-386 (forbes2.txt
lines ~31780-32010; the letter is dated at the foot "the xiii of Aprill 1563"). Aligned with the printed text.

| # | glyphs | plaintext | Forbes |
|---|---|---|---|

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Decryption

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

No. 06, Smith to the Queen, 27 July 1563 (R9250, f. 159)

Sir Thomas Smith to the Queen, 27 July 1563: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (in part; checked against CSP F. 6 no. 1078). In decipher_A.md, Letter 3: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

  `⊓2: L9.4:⊔8⊽L10. ·A ⊚` "at Estaples and Boulogne" `ẁ ζ̣ ⊢̈L⊓4 T̈ 9.5:986.L⊓+4 ö[?] … 2⊓4 … 凸` "whether they mean to surprise [some] town [in] England"

  … `.A ʃ̄ ξ 9.⊓⊔4: :T ⊡` "and he saith that the King of Spain" `L4 4:6.⊔ξ⊔3 T̈ 37 9.7:γω {Lα X5:4[?] α. +○ ϑ. ‡6.5:4:4` "[hath] entered into no such league as France doth bruit"
  `Ẅ ◩ 76. ∞. 96.⊔γL10 467. n̅ ℭ` "with the Pope or other princes, nor never will"
  `.A :T ḥ ⊢̈⊔2ξL2:ω ʃ. ⊓X⊓π4 9.4: T. 4.5:L34 .o 4⊓6:⊓6.7.4` "and that he mindeth nothing against the queen of Navarre"

  `.A 5.Lγ75.L7. :T 2:6.L⊓9.5:6.4: ẃ ξ □⊓2:ω L7. ḥ 9.9L44: Ẅ75:4: …` "and recover that treasure which his father had s[pent] without …" ⟨rest of last line uncertain⟩

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in
England or the Low Countries, no league with the Pope, nothing against the Queen of Navarre, "make himself rich").
The final clause about the father's treasure is not in CSP's summary and is tentative.

## Letter 1 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 27 Jan 1562/3

## Letter 4 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4
DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher
deciphered"). Passages (1)-(2) only numbered; the block is one long stretch. Read as runs; not every group
resolved.

- (1) `464: ö{10: T. :F` — "not only the French[?]" [?]
- (2) `T ⊓⊢̈‡⊔⊓9.10.⊔ξ66. .o ⊡ ṡ 6o[?] ⊢̈L ξ67.` — "the ambassador of Spain send[s] me word"
  `:T ḥ ω⊽[?] ḥ G.` "that he hath good" `⊔ξ5:L7.3:π9.L⊢̈L24:` "advertisement" `:T T :F[?] ṁ: ⟨?⟩ ⊓{ξL7.10. ·A ∞. 9.6:γω L4.5:⊔9…` "that the French make ladders and other such equipages"
  `⊓2: L9.4:⊔8⊽L10. ·A ⊚` "at Estaples and Boulogne" `ẁ ζ̣ ⊢̈L⊓4 T̈ 9.5:986.L⊓+4 ö[?] … 2⊓4 … 凸` "whether they mean to surprise [some] town [in] England"
  … `T. ⊽6◇ γ7 5?24:6.L10:` "[or attempt some place in] the Low Countries"
  … `.A ʃ̄ ξ 9.⊓⊔4: :T ⊡` "and he saith that the King of Spain" `L4 4:6.⊔ξ⊔3 T̈ 37 9.7:γω {Lα X5:4[?] α. +○ ϑ. ‡6.5:4:4` "[hath] entered into no such league as France doth bruit"
  `Ẅ ◩ 76. ∞. 96.⊔γL10 467. n̅ ℭ` "with the Pope or other princes, nor never will"
  `.A :T ḥ ⊢̈⊔2ξL2:ω ʃ. ⊓X⊓π4 9.4: T. 4.5:L34 .o 4⊓6:⊓6.7.4` "and that he mindeth nothing against the queen of Navarre"
  `‡5:4: T̈ ⊽⊔5:4⊔4 2.5:πL4:[?]` "but to live [in quiet]" `·A T̈ ⊢̈⊓θL ω⊔⊢̈ 9.L⊽□ 64 R:` "and to make himself rich"
  `.A 5.Lγ75.L7. :T 2:6.L⊓9.5:6.4: ẃ ξ □⊓2:ω L7. ḥ 9.9L44: Ẅ75:4: …` "and recover that treasure which his father had s[pent] without …" ⟨rest of last line uncertain⟩

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in
England or the Low Countries, no league with the Pope, nothing against the Queen of Navarre, "make himself rich").
The final clause about the father's treasure is not in CSP's summary and is tentative.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

No. 07, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4 (R9250, f. 159)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (large part; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 103). In decipher_A.md, Letter 4: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Decryption

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Letter 4 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4
DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher
deciphered"). Passages (1)-(2) only numbered; the block is one long stretch. Read as runs; not every group
resolved.

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

No. 08, Smith to Cecil, Valence, 1 Sep 1564 (R9252, f. 164)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Valence, 1 Sep 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (28 of 31 passages; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 642). In decipher_D.md, Letter D1 (and the partial first pass in decipher_B.md): glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

| 8 | ♀-box | the Queen's Majesty | ✓ |
| 9 | ♀-box | the Queen's Majesty | ✓ |
| 10 | :F ö | French offer[?] (ö read as offer, not one) | "this French offer" ✓ |

All read, and 1 only in part. Here ":F" means French, not "favour" as in the key.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

(1) and (2) as far as "rid of him" are read and agree with CSP no. 806 almost word for word. The tail of (2) and (3) are

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

No. 10, Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564 (R9252, ff. 164-5)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (about 50 of 62 passages; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 714). Among them: "they feare that she is enclined to this last offer of Lenox". In decipher_C.md, the whole file: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_C.md

# Smith cipher passages, BL Add MS 4136: decipherment C

## Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564 (Forbes heading read so far as "27 Oct"): R9252 p.2 foot and p.3, Add MS 4136 ff.164-165

**Date.** The heading reads "from Avignon, 4 Oct. 1564". The figure is the same looped 4 as the 4 in "1564"; it is not "27".
CSP Foreign vol. 7 has no Smith letter of 27 Oct. It does have **no. 714, Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 1 Oct. 1564, P.S.
4 Oct.** ("Orig., portions in cipher, deciphered"; pp. 217-218). Every firm reading below follows no. 714 §2 in
order, so this is that letter. Crib saved in `csp/v7_pp215-233.html`.

Transcription conventions follow decipher_A.md. Clear nulls (Hull, Calais, Sandwich, Jersey, Antwerpe, bycause) are
dropped. Glyph notes for this letter:
- `ϕ` (a looped kite with a stem) = w (2:ϕ6 = two, three times).
- `{{` / `ſſ` / `ꟿ` = st (papistry, state, stronger, strong).
- `ʋ` (a small open v) = l (litle, gospel, Lenox).
- `ζ` variants (a T with a hook): `ζ` + dot = here (1, 24, 31); `ζ̤` = his/they/them (see each row); `ζ̈` = they/this.
  These dot placements are not stable in Forbes's copy, so they are read from context. Such rows are marked [?].
- `J̣` = is/are; `!` = in/into; `凸`-variant = Earl (before Murray, Lennox, Warwick; plain 凸 = England in 14).
- `B:` = Bishop; `C̄` = Congregation; `M̅` = marriage (33, 41, 48); `ṃ` = marry; `Ṗ` privy, `.P` practise.

## Summary
- Letter identified: the heading's date is **4 Oct 1564**, not 27 Oct; it is CSP Foreign vii **no. 714** (Avignon,
  1 Oct with P.S. 4 Oct 1564, "portions in cipher, deciphered"). The ciphered passages carry §2 of the calendar
  entry in order, from "Their designs here are two" to "this last offer of Lennox's".
- 62 numbered passages (there is no (6)), plus an unnumbered line (12) and a run-on (29b).
  - About 50 read firmly: 1-4, 7, 9-11, 13-16, 19-21, 24-29b, 31-33, 35-38, 40-51, 53-57, 59-63.
  - About 10 read in part or uncertain: 5, 8, 12, 17, 18, 22, 23, 30, 34, 39, 52, 58.
  - 22 and 23 ("by little and little and by … great") have no counterpart in the CSP summary and are only partly read.
- Code words used here beyond decipher_A: ϕ = w; ſſ/ꟿ = st; ʋ = l; J̣ = is/are; ! = in/into. The ζ family
  (here/his/they/this/thing) and the Earl sign carry the most doubt, because Forbes's dots are not consistent.
- The crib was used only to check readings. The spelled-out words (designs, papistry, gospel, Italian, contreband,
  interrupt, Glasgow, embassador, profession, Poictiers, bushoprick, reserved, dispatcheth, espials, negligent,
  anker, Robert, cardinals, Guise, stronger, Babtista, London, dispache, enclined, Lenox) come from the glyphs alone.
Decryption

decipher_C.md

# Smith cipher passages, BL Add MS 4136: decipherment C

## Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564 (Forbes heading read so far as "27 Oct"): R9252 p.2 foot and p.3, Add MS 4136 ff.164-165

**Date.** The heading reads "from Avignon, 4 Oct. 1564". The figure is the same looped 4 as the 4 in "1564"; it is not "27".
CSP Foreign vol. 7 has no Smith letter of 27 Oct. It does have **no. 714, Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 1 Oct. 1564, P.S.
4 Oct.** ("Orig., portions in cipher, deciphered"; pp. 217-218). Every firm reading below follows no. 714 §2 in
order, so this is that letter. Crib saved in `csp/v7_pp215-233.html`.

Transcription conventions follow decipher_A.md. Clear nulls (Hull, Calais, Sandwich, Jersey, Antwerpe, bycause) are
dropped. Glyph notes for this letter:
- `ϕ` (a looped kite with a stem) = w (2:ϕ6 = two, three times).
- `{{` / `ſſ` / `ꟿ` = st (papistry, state, stronger, strong).
- `ʋ` (a small open v) = l (litle, gospel, Lenox).
- `ζ` variants (a T with a hook): `ζ` + dot = here (1, 24, 31); `ζ̤` = his/they/them (see each row); `ζ̈` = they/this.
  These dot placements are not stable in Forbes's copy, so they are read from context. Such rows are marked [?].
- `J̣` = is/are; `!` = in/into; `凸`-variant = Earl (before Murray, Lennox, Warwick; plain 凸 = England in 14).
- `B:` = Bishop; `C̄` = Congregation; `M̅` = marriage (33, 41, 48); `ṃ` = marry; `Ṗ` privy, `.P` practise.

## Summary
- Letter identified: the heading's date is **4 Oct 1564**, not 27 Oct; it is CSP Foreign vii **no. 714** (Avignon,
  1 Oct with P.S. 4 Oct 1564, "portions in cipher, deciphered"). The ciphered passages carry §2 of the calendar
  entry in order, from "Their designs here are two" to "this last offer of Lennox's".
- 62 numbered passages (there is no (6)), plus an unnumbered line (12) and a run-on (29b).
  - About 50 read firmly: 1-4, 7, 9-11, 13-16, 19-21, 24-29b, 31-33, 35-38, 40-51, 53-57, 59-63.
  - About 10 read in part or uncertain: 5, 8, 12, 17, 18, 22, 23, 30, 34, 39, 52, 58.
  - 22 and 23 ("by little and little and by … great") have no counterpart in the CSP summary and are only partly read.
- Code words used here beyond decipher_A: ϕ = w; ſſ/ꟿ = st; ʋ = l; J̣ = is/are; ! = in/into. The ζ family
  (here/his/they/this/thing) and the Earl sign carry the most doubt, because Forbes's dots are not consistent.
- The crib was used only to check readings. The spelled-out words (designs, papistry, gospel, Italian, contreband,
  interrupt, Glasgow, embassador, profession, Poictiers, bushoprick, reserved, dispatcheth, espials, negligent,
  anker, Robert, cardinals, Guise, stronger, Babtista, London, dispache, enclined, Lenox) come from the glyphs alone.

No. 11, Smith to Cecil, Marseilles, 13 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Marseilles, 13 Nov 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (in part; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 793). In decipher_D.md, Letter D3: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

Read almost entirely. A few code signs are open (lines 4, 6, 7). The reading agrees with CSP no. 815 throughout.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

No. 12, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 20 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 20 Nov 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (large part; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 806). A marginal gloss spells out "Enos" = Ambassador. In decipher_D.md, Letter D4: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Letter D1: Smith to Cecil, Valence 1 Sep 1564
DECODE R9252 p.2 (IMG_R9252_I43269_P2), Add MS 4136 f.164. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 642 ("portions in cipher,
deciphered"), §3.

| # | glyphs (normalized) | plaintext | CSP 642 agreement |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⩓ | Scotland | "straighter alliance betwixt Scotland and France" ✓ |
| 2 | ḧ-like sign | ⟨code⟩ | — |
| 3 | ⩓ | Scotland | ✓ |
| 4 | T· ⊓⊢̈π2:9: | the amity | "break the amity" ✓ |
| 5 | 凸 | England | ✓ |
| 6 | ⩓ | Scotland | ✓ |
| 7 | :L ·o ⊢̈5:5·6·⊓8: | Lord of Murray | "the Earl of Murray" ✓ |
| 8 | 8·7:∝ꟿ :L ·A G̈ M· | such lords and great men | ✓ |
| 9 | b: T· G̈ □⊔6:5·L6·9· ·o 5·L{πXπ62 / T: | be the great favourers of religion there | ✓ |
| 10 | T̈ θ·[?] ⊕[?] G̈ L22:L6·87·π9·L ·T ξπ9:L[?] | to do[?] some[?] great enterprise that wise[?] | "to do some great enterprise that way" ✓ (θ·, ⊕ read from context) |
| 11 | T· ⩓(Scots) ḣ[?] | the Scots … | "all the Scots and others" ✓ |
| 12 | ·o T· 5·L{πXπ62 | of the religion | ✓ |
| 13 | f M̈[?] ï:L·o ⊢̈5:5·6·⊓8: | for … [the] Lord of Murray | "marvellous fear for the Lord of Murray" ✓ (M̈ ⟨?⟩) |
| 14 | ⴶ ω522:ꝏ9: ξπLξ ·A ḧ 8·622L10 / ξL6·L 2:⊓θLȝ | [the Earl of] Huntly died and his sonnes were taken | ✓ (ⴶ = Earl ⟨?⟩) |
| 15 | B: ḣ | bishops here | "These bishops here" ✓ |
| 16 | ◩ ∝66:49·L{ | the Pope's counsel | ✓ |
| 17 | b: a· ‡6:9·10: a· m b: :a | be as busy as may be … | "are as busy as may be" ✓ |
| 18 | T· :L ·o ꝏ⊓ξπ4X2:54 | the Lord of Lethington | "suspect Lethington" ✓ (spelling of the ⊓ξ pair [?]) |
| 19 | J̣ :a ‡6·6:Xπ10· | ⟨J̣ = whose brother?⟩ at Bruges | "whose brother is at Bruges" ✓ |
| 20 | T· B: / ·o X{⊔9·X6:4 | the Bishop of Glasgow | ✓ |
| 21 | 8L4γ62 | pension (pencon) | ✓ |
| 22 | ï:L ·o / ꝏ⊓ξπ4X2:54 ·o T· ♁ | [the] Lord of Lethington, of the French King | "promised to Lethington by the French King" ✓ |
| 23 | ·o T· ⊡ | of the King of Spain | ✓ |
| 24 | ▷ | the Queen of Scots | ✓ |
| 25 | T̈ M̈ :L 5·5‡L6·4: | to marry Lord Robert | ✓ (M̈ = marry, from context) |
| 26 | 凸 ·A ⩓ | England and Scotland | ✓ |
| 27 | ẓ T̈ T 66:{ξ / ◩ | ⟨reduce?⟩ to the old Pope | "reduce the realm to the old Pope" ✓ |
| 28 | 凸 ·A ⩓ | England and Scotland | ✓ |
| 29 | M̈[?] :L ·o ⊢̈5:5·6·⊓8: | ⟨M̈⟩ Lord of Murray | "give Murray warning" ✓ |
| 30 | ·o T· :q ·o ⊡ | of the Queen of Spain | ✓ |
| 31 | ⊡ T̈ ▷ | the King of Spain to the Queen of Scotland | ✓ |

28 of 31 read in full or nearly so. (2), (13) and (29) are only partly read. This replaces the partial start in decipher_B.

## Letter D3: Smith to Cecil, Marseilles 13 Nov 1564
DECODE R9253 (IMG_R9253_I43273_P), f.166 top. CSP vol. 7 no. 793 ("portions in cipher, deciphered").

| # | glyphs | plaintext | CSP 793 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⩓ | Scotland | "Still calls upon him for Scotland" ✓ |
| 2 | 1 a· G ⊔ 2:6·‡ꝏL | … so[?] great a trouble | "never here in so great a trouble" ✓ (the word "trouble" is short by o/u) |
| 3 | m̄10· | ⟨m̄⟩s | "the posts come continually"? ✗ unread |
| 4 | ! 凸 ·A ⩓ | ⟨!⟩ England and Scotland | "post for Scotland" ~ |
| 5 | ⩓ ·1 ⊢̈6:{{ ⊔̲ :k ·o 1̲ ḣ X6L2:ω :b ⊙ | Scotland … ⟨…⟩ he goeth by ⟨⊙⟩ | "who goes by Dieppe" ✓ in part. ⊙ is Calais in the key, and whether it stands for Dieppe here is [?] |
| 6 | :b ·ꝺ | by ⟨sea?⟩ | "or otherwise by sea" ~ |
| 7 | :b T· θ-sign | by the Cardinal of Lorraine | ✓ |
| 8 | T m· ʒ̣ ω62:L | the matter is hote | "The matter is hot" ✓ |
| 9 | 1̲ ʒ̣ γωL□L T· m̄ m· / ‡Lꝏ ⊔9⊓8:L | ⟨…⟩ chief[?] the marriage matter … bell[e] away[?] | "chiefly the marriage matter… who shall bear the belle away" ✓ in part |
| 10 | ꞔ̲ :ꝏꝏ | ⟨code⟩ | — |
| 11 | T | the | — |
| 12 | :b ꞔ̲ 1 ξ⊔6·L 8·8Lꝏθ | by whom[?] I dare speak | "his men… by whom he dare speak" ✓ |

Read in part: 1, 2, 7, 8, 12 are firm, and 5, 6 and 9 are partly read.

## Letter D4: Smith to Cecil, Arles 20 Nov 1564
R9253, f.166 middle. CSP vol. 7 no. 806.

| # | glyphs | plaintext | CSP 806 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ẗ ḣ ⊔ξ⊓⊢̈ ϖ6:⊢̈L / ·1 p· :b m̲ ⊔ꝏπ2X2:6310· ℓ̲ ξωπγω ẏ :s M ξ⊔10· ẅ / :ʒba ẅ ḣ ξL8·56·6:Lξ 1 ṙ n̲ f: | this [bearer] Adam Hume, I p[erceive] by Mr Alingtons letter which you sent me, was well rewarded. What he deserved I can not tell | ✓ |
| 2 | 1̲ ξ⊔10 ḣ :a T ⊔· ḣ ξ⊔9· ö ·o 2:ωL⊢̈ / T̈ ꝏ⊔2: T· ꞔ̇ :a T / :W Ẅ ꝏπL10· ·A □66·XLξ π26:L3γ⊔649· / m:da T·.o ⊢̈⊔ξL 5:π7· 2π∝ω6{⊔8· Ä 66· A: 2:6Xπ3:ϖL7: ⊢̈⊔ξ / ! T Enos ·o ⩓10· / ḣ ξ⊔8:L A: ẗ 2:9:⊢̈L | it was he, at the [least] he was one of them, which with lies and forged invencions to let the peace at the making thereof made Sir Nicholas almost or altogether mad. [He] lay[?] all this tyme [with] the Ambassador of Scotland | ✓ (order: the "to let the peace" line stands before :W on the page) |
| 2 cont. | ḣ ꟿ̲ a· 1 T̈ 8·6:9·8Lγ2:bo ḣ T̈ b: :s F: T 85·62:L⊔‡⊔ / 23:L10 ·o ⩓ T Enos J̣ ω6ꝏꝏL f T· 8⊔9π{{L10· ·A / a·: a· ḣ γ66:{ξ ·A Ẅ⊔ T J̣ X{⊔ξ T̈ b: 6·πξ ·o ḣ | who[?] … suspecteth him to be sent from the Protestants of Scotland. The Ambassador is wholly for the Papistes, and … glad to be rid of him | ✓ |
| 2 cont. | 1 ṙ 6:4ξL6·{{⊔3ξ n̅ ẗ :b ϖ6:⊢̈L 8·⊢̈⊔{{ L6·7·⊓4ξ / ẅ :a 1 ⊢̈⊔6·6:L{ | I [?] understand[?] now this by Hume['s] small errand … at I marvel[?] | not in CSP (tentative) |
| 3 | 8·5⊓π‡{9: ḣ h· ė ξ⊔6·4ξo / 3: ẓ ξ7·9:9ξ 96·6γ6:6·Lξ ẓ ·o ḣ | prob[a]bly[?] he had … warned[?] … procured[?] … of him | not in CSP; unread in large part |

(1) and (2) as far as "rid of him" are read and agree with CSP no. 806 almost word for word. The tail of (2) and (3) are
tentative.

Read in part.

## Letter D6: Smith to Cecil, Arles 24 Nov 1564
R9253, f.166 bottom (a continuous block of 12 lines). CSP vol. 7 no. 815 ("chiefly in cipher, deciphered").

| line | glyphs | plaintext | CSP 815 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊤|(Card. Lorraine) ·A T ⊖|(Condé) .h h. γ62□L6·L39· ·A □⊓⊢̈π{⊔⊔7· ‡⊓226:L | The Cardinal of Lorraine and the Prince of Condé have had conferens and familiar bankette- | ✓ |
| 2 | 2:da 2:6Xπ3:ωL6· :a 8·5π⊓6310· ·A :a 2⊔⊓L⊔6: ꝏL | -ting togither at Soissons and at Nanteuil le | "at Soissons and at Nisseau le Château" ✓ |
| 3 | γω⊓{{L⊔7: :W ξ⊔10 T ꞔ⊔2:L ω10·[?] ·o ‡6:6·‡639· ·A A1 T̈ | chasteau, which was the late ⟨Cardinal?⟩ of Burbons, and all to | "which belonged to the late Cardinal of Bourbon" ✓ |
| 4 | ·h ⊔⊢̈πꝏ ẓ · ẗ J̣ ⊢̈6:γωL 262:Lξ ḣ ẓ̣ ·o X5:π9·L | ⟨…⟩ this is much noted here. They of Guise | ✓ |
| 5 | θ· γ6⊢̈8{Lπ3L ·T A: ℓ̲10 ·T γ9·⊢̈L F: T· ⌐ω ·A T· | do complaine that all letters that come from the Cardinal, and the | "complain that all letters from the said Cardinal are opened" ✓ |
| 6 | ꝏ66·L8:25π10 b: :oba ·A ξ9·θbo 1 86·π2γπ9⊔{{10: :a | ⟨…⟩ be opened[?] and ⟨…⟩ principally at | ✓ in part |
| 7 | 8⊓6·π10· ·A ξ8:629· ẗ :a ẗ F̄ ·A T· L⊢̈‡⊔⊓2:57· | Paris and Lyons. This at this ⟨F̄⟩, and the Embassatour | "The Ambassador of Scotland…" ✓ |
| 8 | ⩓ ·A T· ⌐ω ·A ⊢̈⊔ξ⊔⊢̈L ξL Xꞔ π9·L b: ξ⊔9:{8 1 G̈ f | of Scotland and the Cardinal and Madame de Guise be daily in great | ✓ |
| 9 | ξL8Lγω410· T̈ ·A F: ⩓ ·A T̈ ·A F: T ⊖ ·A A: :b ·ꝺ | dispeches to and from Scotland and to and from the Cardinal of Lorraine, and all by ⟨…⟩ | ✓ |
| 10 | ẗ ḥ ϖ6:⊢̈L J̣ A: 2:6Xπ3:ωL6· π ξπξ⊔2X2:64 | this bearer Hume is altogether a Lidington | "altogether a Lethington" ✓ |
| 11 | ẘ ẏ .ſ F ξ6‡‡{L 1 ·ꝺ ⊔□6·⊔πLξ 57· ꝏ: 3:6·8:8{L | whom you shall find double, I am afraid, or rather triple | ✓ |
| 12 | .h da C· ·A 8L2γπ6310· ·o A: 2:ω67·L 86·π2γL10· | having ⟨C·⟩ and pencions of all three princes | "having pensions of all three princes" ✓ |

Read almost entirely. A few code signs are open (lines 4, 6, 7). The reading agrees with CSP no. 815 throughout.

| 6 | A: ẗ ξ̣ / 1̣ T· m̄ ‡̇ ♀ ·A ▷ | all this house[?] … in the marriage between the Duke of Guise and the Queen of Scots | "All that house… the marriage between the Duke of Guise and the Queen of Scotland" ✓ |

Read in large part.

## Letter D8: Smith to Cecil, Toulouse 9 Feb 1564/5
R9254 p.1, f.167. CSP vol. 7 no. 979.

| # | glyphs | plaintext | CSP 979 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | n̲ ẗ m̤ 1 T· ë / ∝π64·⊔ ∝53ξ7{L⊔9:4∝L | ⟨…⟩ in the ⟨…⟩ …cion and condoleance | "occasions offered by congratulation and condolence" ✓ |
| 2 | γ52X5·⊔2:6:ξπ | congratuli[ation] | ✓ |
| 3 | ·o T· :F | of the French | "this tickling of the French" ✓ |
| 4 | ·o π22:L5·5:ꞔξ⌐ | of intervewe | "desire of interview" ✓ |
| 5 | ♀-box | the Queen's Majesty | "letters to the Queen" ✓ |
| 6 | flag sign | ⟨the Emperor's house / Charles of Austria?⟩ | "understanding of Charles of Austria" ✓ |
| 7 | :F | the French | "The French have time enough" ✓ |
| 8 | ♀-box | the Queen's Majesty | ✓ |
| 9 | ♀-box | the Queen's Majesty | ✓ |
| 10 | :F ö | French offer[?] (ö read as offer, not one) | "this French offer" ✓ |

All read, and 1 only in part. Here ":F" means French, not "favour" as in the key.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

No. 13, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 22 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 22 Nov 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (in part (two signs); checked against CSP F. 7 no. 808). In decipher_D.md, Letter D5: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).

  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

No. 14, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 24 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 24 Nov 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all but a few signs; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 815). The Cardinal of Lorraine and the Prince of Condé banqueting together at Soissons and Nanteuil, the Guise complaint that the Cardinal's letters are opened at Paris and Lyons, and the bearer Hume "altogether a Lidington ... having pencions of all three princes". In decipher_D.md, Letter D6: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
