## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

No. 15, Smith to Cecil, Narbonne, 7 Jan 1564/5 (R9254, f. 167)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Narbonne, 7 Jan 1564/5: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (large part; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 916). In decipher_D.md, Letter D7: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Letter D8: Smith to Cecil, Toulouse 9 Feb 1564/5

| # | glyphs | plaintext | CSP 979 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | n̲ ẗ m̤ 1 T· ë / ∝π64·⊔ ∝53ξ7{L⊔9:4∝L | ⟨…⟩ in the ⟨…⟩ …cion and condoleance | "occasions offered by congratulation and condolence" ✓ |
| 2 | γ52X5·⊔2:6:ξπ | congratuli[ation] | ✓ |
| 3 | ·o T· :F | of the French | "this tickling of the French" ✓ |

All read, and 1 only in part. Here ":F" means French, not "favour" as in the key.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

No. 16, Smith to Cecil, Toulouse, 9 Feb 1564/5 (R9254, f. 167)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Toulouse, 9 Feb 1564/5: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all 10 passages; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 979). In decipher_D.md, Letter D8: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

No. 17, Smith to Cecil, 22 Mar 1565/6 (R9250, ff. 159-60)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, 22 Mar 1565/6: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (about 12 of 27 passages; checked against CSP F. 8 no. 208). In decipher_A.md, Letter 5: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Decryption

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

No. 18, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 29 Apr 1566 (R9250, f. 160)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 29 Apr 1566: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all 5 passages; checked against CSP F. 8 no. 327). In decipher_A.md, Letter 6: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

  `.A :T ḥ ⊢̈⊔2ξL2:ω ʃ. ⊓X⊓π4 9.4: T. 4.5:L34 .o 4⊓6:⊓6.7.4` "and that he mindeth nothing against the queen of Navarre"

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in

## Letter 3 — Smith to the Queen, 27 July 1563
DECODE R9250 P1, Add MS 4136 f.159. Not in Forbes as such; CSP Foreign vol. 6 no. 1078 (Smith to the Queen,
Valmont 28 July 1563, §5: the Earl of Warwick's letter "does not answer whether he be able to keep it, or for what
time, nor what men he has in it"). Forbes's date 27 July may be the date of drafting; the content fits no. 1078.

| # | glyphs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊢̈9:(my) ⊽76.ξ .o ◇—◇⊓6.◇πγθ | my Lord of Warwick |
| 2 | ·◇[?] ġ[?] b: ⊓‡⊽L T̈ θL9 ⊓2:76.4 6yf 377:f[?] / Ẍ[?] 4.L6.⊢̈L[?] ·A 465. ö ⊢̈L4 ḥ ω⊓3: ϖπ4 ⊔4: | … be able to keep [the] town … [for how long] … and how many men he hath in it [?] — only "be able to keep" and "men he hath" are firm; rest ⟨uncertain⟩ |

Read in part; the firm words match CSP's summary.

## Letter 4 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4
DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher
deciphered"). Passages (1)-(2) only numbered; the block is one long stretch. Read as runs; not every group
resolved.

- (1) `464: ö{10: T. :F` — "not only the French[?]" [?]
- (2) `T ⊓⊢̈‡⊔⊓9.10.⊔ξ66. .o ⊡ ṡ 6o[?] ⊢̈L ξ67.` — "the ambassador of Spain send[s] me word"
  `:T ḥ ω⊽[?] ḥ G.` "that he hath good" `⊔ξ5:L7.3:π9.L⊢̈L24:` "advertisement" `:T T :F[?] ṁ: ⟨?⟩ ⊓{ξL7.10. ·A ∞. 9.6:γω L4.5:⊔9…` "that the French make ladders and other such equipages"
  `⊓2: L9.4:⊔8⊽L10. ·A ⊚` "at Estaples and Boulogne" `ẁ ζ̣ ⊢̈L⊓4 T̈ 9.5:986.L⊓+4 ö[?] … 2⊓4 … 凸` "whether they mean to surprise [some] town [in] England"
  … `T. ⊽6◇ γ7 5?24:6.L10:` "[or attempt some place in] the Low Countries"
  … `.A ʃ̄ ξ 9.⊓⊔4: :T ⊡` "and he saith that the King of Spain" `L4 4:6.⊔ξ⊔3 T̈ 37 9.7:γω {Lα X5:4[?] α. +○ ϑ. ‡6.5:4:4` "[hath] entered into no such league as France doth bruit"
  `Ẅ ◩ 76. ∞. 96.⊔γL10 467. n̅ ℭ` "with the Pope or other princes, nor never will"
  `.A :T ḥ ⊢̈⊔2ξL2:ω ʃ. ⊓X⊓π4 9.4: T. 4.5:L34 .o 4⊓6:⊓6.7.4` "and that he mindeth nothing against the queen of Navarre"
  `‡5:4: T̈ ⊽⊔5:4⊔4 2.5:πL4:[?]` "but to live [in quiet]" `·A T̈ ⊢̈⊓θL ω⊔⊢̈ 9.L⊽□ 64 R:` "and to make himself rich"
  `.A 5.Lγ75.L7. :T 2:6.L⊓9.5:6.4: ẃ ξ □⊓2:ω L7. ḥ 9.9L44: Ẅ75:4: …` "and recover that treasure which his father had s[pent] without …" ⟨rest of last line uncertain⟩

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in
England or the Low Countries, no league with the Pope, nothing against the Queen of Navarre, "make himself rich").
The final clause about the father's treasure is not in CSP's summary and is tentative.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

No. 19, Smith to Cecil, 15 Oct 1562 (R9236, f. 140)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, 15 Oct 1562: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all 6 passages; checked against Forbes ii 119). "break with you and to make war", hostages, Rollet and Vyllemort. In decipher_E.md, section 1: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

decipher_E.md

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)

| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

Conventions as in ./decipher_A.md. Clear-English nulls (don, and, lyn, poole, boston, dover, garnesey) are left out.
New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

## Block 1: Smith to the Queen, 4 Nov 1563 (7 lines)

| line | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ξL ⊢̈7 2 4:⊓πX 2 | de Montaign[e] [?] |
| 2 | ʃ ·T ‖ 8̈[?] 9.8L⊔θ4 .o / ◇—◇ω79.4 γ⊓6.4: 4 .o ξπ7.6γωL | is that I [?] speake of / whose c[ha]rte[?] of dis[?]che [?dispatch] |

## Block 2: advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562 (upright, below the signature)

|---|---|---|

## Unresolved
- block 1: 8̈ (l.2); c(h)arte/dis?che group (l.2); barred ⊖ at start of l.3; ƈ with bar (l.5, a person, sender from Rouen?);
  ẇ. and ẇ: (l.6); ξ̣ with dot (read as "he"/"him", uncertain); ‖ before "wrote" in l.5 (maybe part of ⊕|).
- block 2: mostly unread; the name in (1), the sign in (2), the words in (3) and the whole last line.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

Conventions as in ./decipher_A.md. Clear-English nulls (don, and, lyn, poole, boston, dover, garnesey) are left out.
New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

  ẇ. and ẇ: (l.6); ξ̣ with dot (read as "he"/"him", uncertain); ‖ before "wrote" in l.5 (maybe part of ⊕|).
- block 2: mostly unread; the name in (1), the sign in (2), the words in (3) and the whole last line.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

Conventions as in ./decipher_A.md. Clear-English nulls (don, and, lyn, poole, boston, dover, garnesey) are left out.
New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

No. 22, Smith to the Queen, 4 Nov 1563 (R9236, f. 140)

Sir Thomas Smith to the Queen, 4 Nov 1563: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all; checked against CSP F. 6 no. 1366). Montaigne's carte of La Rochelle, letters to my Lord Robert and Mr Secretarie. In decipher_E.md, late block 1: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

Conventions as in ./decipher_A.md. Clear-English nulls (don, and, lyn, poole, boston, dover, garnesey) are left out.
New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

## Block 2: advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562 (upright, below the signature)

## Unresolved

  ẇ. and ẇ: (l.6); ξ̣ with dot (read as "he"/"him", uncertain); ‖ before "wrote" in l.5 (maybe part of ⊕|).
- block 2: mostly unread; the name in (1), the sign in (2), the words in (3) and the whole last line.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

No. 23, Smith advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562/3 (R9236, f. 140)

Sir Thomas Smith advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562/3: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (passage (2) on the queen of Navarre; the rest fragments; checked against not found in Forbes or CSP F. 6). In decipher_E.md, late block 2: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

decipher_E.md

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".

| 5 | 10.Lγ6.L4:⊓7.‖4 T̈ ƈ[?] F: ⊕ ‖[?] ʢ6.74:L .o T. 9.⊓⊢̈L m. | Secretarie; and to [?] from Rouen [?] wrote of that same matter |
| 6 | T. ẇ.[?] l̲10. ξ̣[?] γ⊓6.шLξ ξ. 8.L⊽□4 .A ‖4 ·T □L⊓3:4 ẇ:[?] .o ‖ | that [?] letters [he?] carried himselfe, and in that feate [whereof?] I |
| 7 | ʢ6.53:L 10.7⊢̈ ʢω⊓4: ‖ 4:ωπγθ4 ξ̣[?] γ⊓4 ξ̣[?] ‡5:4: 2:ωπ9. / ⊓4: 3:ϖπ10. 2:10:⊢̈4 ‖9. ⊢̈69.4: 2LγL9.8.⊓6.‖4 | wrote somwhat, I thinke [he?] can do; but this at this tyme is most necessarie |

Running text: "...de Montaign[e] is that I [?] speake of, whose [c(h)arte?] of [dispatch?] [?] I have by here with and under
another pretence. I have given [him] letters to my Lord Robert and Mr Secretarie, and to [?] from Rouen [?] wrote of that same
matter, that [?] letters [he] carried himselfe; and in that feate [whereof] I wrote somwhat I thinke [he] can do. But this at
this tyme is most necessarie."

## Block 2: advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562 (upright, below the signature)

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | o:(Monsieur) ξL ſ.[?]6:9: | Monsieur de [L?]uy[?] (name unread) |
| 2 | :q.(queen) .o ♁-cross[?] | the queen of [Navarre?] |
| 3 | :q. ξ̈(hath) m̲[?] L8T̈[?] 7.π5:3: 8̈[?] | the queen hath [?] ... (unread) |
| last | ξ ‡⊓2ξ T. ⊕ .a γ⊓9: Ẅ ξ ω. ξ ⊓10..H | [I?] band[?] that Rouen as [?] with ... (unread) |

## Unresolved
- block 1: 8̈ (l.2); c(h)arte/dis?che group (l.2); barred ⊖ at start of l.3; ƈ with bar (l.5, a person, sender from Rouen?);
  ẇ. and ẇ: (l.6); ξ̣ with dot (read as "he"/"him", uncertain); ‖ before "wrote" in l.5 (maybe part of ⊕|).
- block 2: mostly unread; the name in (1), the sign in (2), the words in (3) and the whole last line.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.
